Relative Unification in Intuitionistic Logic: Towards provability logic of HA
Mojtaba Mojtahedi

Abstract
This paper studies relative unification and admissibility in the intuitionistic logic. We generalize results of [ Ghilardi, 1999 ; Iemhoff, 2001a ] and prove them relative in 𝖭𝖭𝖨𝖫 ⁡ ( 𝗉𝖺𝗋 ) {{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})} propositions, the class of propositions with No Nested Implications in the Left made up from parameters. The main application of such generalization is to characterize provability logic of Heyting Arithmetic HA and prove its decidability [ Mojtahedi, 2022 ] .



1
Introduction

Silvio Ghilardi
[
Ghilardi, 1999
;
Ghilardi, 2000
]
studies
unification in propositional logics. More precisely, he
describes all solutions for

A(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\leftrightarrow\top

within a background logic like intuitionistic logic
{\sf IPC}
or a modal logic containing
{\sf K4}
. By a solution we mean a substitution

\theta
such that
\theta(A\leftrightarrow\top)
holds.

On the other hand, we have a related question for
decidability/characterization
of admissible rules of
{\sf IPC}
.
A rule
A/B
is admissible to a logic
{\sf L}
if

{\sf L}\vdash\theta(A)
implies
{\sf L}\vdash\theta(B)

for every substitution
\theta
.
Despite classical logic,
in which every admissible rule is also derivable,
the case of modal logic and intuitionistic logic are not trivial.
Probably the first such underivable admissible rule for

{\sf IPC}
is the following
[
Harrop, 1960
]
:

\neg A\to(B\vee C)

(\neg A\to B)\vee(\neg A\to C)

Using the tools and results in
[
Ghilardi, 1999
]
, Rosalie Iemhoff
proves the completeness of a base for

all
admissible rules of
{\sf IPC}

[
Iemhoff, 2001b
;
Iemhoff, 2001a
]
, which previously conjectured by
de Jongh and Visser.
Decidability of admissibility for
{\sf IPC}
was already known

[
Rybakov, 1987b
;
Rybakov, 1992
;
Rybakov, 1997
]
.
There are
similar results in some modal logics extending
{\sf K4}
both for
unification
[
Ghilardi, 2000
]
and
admissibility
[
Jeřábek, 2005
;
Iemhoff and Metcalfe, 2009
]
.

There is yet another related notion,
preservativity
,
an intuitionistic alternate for the classical notion of interpretability
or conservativity
[
Iemhoff, 2003
;
Visser, 2002
]
.
Preservativity is a binary relation
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}B

defined as “
\Gamma\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}A
implies
\Gamma\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}B
”.
Albert Visser in
[
Visser, 2002
]
shows that
{\sf NNIL}
-preservativity and admissibility are tightly related, in which
{\sf NNIL}
, is the class of No
Nested Implications in the Left, introduced in

[
Visser et al., 1995
]
and more elaborated in
[
Visser, 2002
]
.
This class of propositions are proved to be helpful in the realm of
intuitionistic logic. A crucial
result concerning
{\sf NNIL}
appeared in
[
Visser, 2002
]
is to provide
an algorithm that takes
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
and returns its best

{\sf NNIL}
approximation
A^{*}
from below, i.e.,
\vdash A^{*}\rightarrow A
and
for all
{\sf NNIL}
formulae
B
such that
\vdash B\rightarrow A
, we have

\vdash B\rightarrow A^{*}
.
Later in
section

4.5
we also provide an algorithm
which computes
A^{\star}
, the best
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
-approximation of
A
from below.

The main work of current paper is to extend

[
Ghilardi, 1999
;
Iemhoff, 2001a
]
and prove their results relative in

{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
propositions, the class of No Nested Implications in the Left
[
Visser et al., 1995
]
which are made up from set of atomic parameters
{\sf par}
.
First we imitate
[
Ghilardi, 1999
]
and study
projectivity and extendibility relative in
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
-propositions
(
theorem

3.12
). This
will lead us to a relativised version of projective approximations
(
theorem

3.27
).
Then we take a route similar to
[
Iemhoff, 2001a
]
and
provide a base called
{{\sf AR}_{{\sf par}}}\!\,
, for
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
-admissibility of
{\sf IPC}

and prove its completeness (
theorem

4.15
).
This last result together with
[
Ardeshir and Mojtahedi, 2018
;
Mojtahedi, 2021
]
,
lead us to the characterization and decidability
of provability logic of Heyting arithmetic
HA
,
which is splitted to another manuscript
[
Mojtahedi, 2022
]
.

Finally we axiomatize two interesting preservativity predicates

\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf IPC}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}\
: first when
\Gamma
is considered as the set of

{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
-projective propositions (this is same as projectivity relative in
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
, as defined in
section

3.1
), and second when
\Gamma:={{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
.

2
Preliminary definitions and facts

This section is devoted to preliminaries and conventions.
Among other well-known notions, we
define
{\sf NNIL}
propositions, admissibility, preservativity and greatest lower bounds.

2.1
propositional language

The propositional language
\mathcal{L}_{0}
includes connectives

\vee
,
\wedge
,
\to
and
\bot
. Negation
\neg
is defined
as
\neg A:=A\to\bot
and
\top:=\neg\bot
. By default we assume that
\mathcal{L}_{0}
includes
finite set of atomic variables
{\sf var}

and also finite set of atomic parameters

{\sf par}
.
The union
{\sf var}\cup{\sf par}
is
annotated as
{\sf atom}
, the set of atomics.
We use

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle p\hfil

and

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle q\hfil

as a finite set or list of parameters
and

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle x\hfil

and

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle y\hfil

for a finite set or list of variables.
Finite lists or sets of atomics are annotated by

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle a\hfil

and

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle b\hfil

.
We use
x
,
y
and
z
(possibly with subscripts) as meta-variables for variables and also
p
,
q
and
r
(possibly with subscripts) for parameters. Also
a
,
b
and
c

(again possibly with subscripts) are used for both atomic variables and parameters.

Let
\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern be a list of atomics and

\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle B\hfil$\crcr}}}=B_{1},\ldots,B_{n}
. Then
A[\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle B\hfil$\crcr}}}]

indicate the simultaneous substitution of

B_{i}
for
a_{i}
in
A
.

We also use the notation
\mathcal{L}_{0}(X)
to indicate the language of all boolean combinations of propositions in
X
.
We consider
{\sf IPC}
as the intuitionistic propositional logic

[
Troelstra and van Dalen, 1988
]
and

\vdash
indicates derivability in
{\sf IPC}
.
All propositional logics considered in this paper are assumed
to be closed under (1) modus ponens and (2) substitutions

2.2
Substitutions

A substitution
\theta
is a function on propositional language
\mathcal{L}_{0}

which commutes with all connectives, i.e.

•

\theta(B\circ C)=\theta(B)\circ\theta(C)
for every
\circ\in\{\vee,\wedge,\to\}
.

•

\theta(\bot)=\bot
.

By default we assume that all substitutions are identity on the set

{\sf par}
of parameters.
We say that a substitution is
general
,
if we relax this condition on
{\sf par}
and allow the parameters to be substituted as well.

2.3
Kripke models for intuitionistic logic

A Kripke model for intuitionistic logic, is a triple

\mathcal{K}=(W,\prec,\mathrel{V})
with following properties:

•

W\neq\emptyset
.

•

(W,\prec)
is a partial order (transitive and irreflexive). We write
\preccurlyeq
for the reflexive closure of
\prec
.

•

\mathrel{V}
is the valuation on atomics,
i.e.
V\subseteq W\times{\sf atom}
.

•

w\preccurlyeq u
and
w\mathrel{V}a
implies
u\mathrel{V}a
for every
w,u\in W
and
a\in{\sf atom}
.

The valuation
\mathrel{V}
may be extended to include all propositions as follows:

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash a
iff
w\mathrel{V}a
, for
a\in{\sf atom}
.

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A\wedge B
iff
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
and

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B
.

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A\vee B
iff
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
or

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B
.

•

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A\to B
iff for every
u\succcurlyeq w
if we have

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
then

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash B
.

We also define the following notions for Kripke models:

•

Finite:
if
W
is a finite set.

•

Rooted:
if there is some node
w_{0}\in W

such that
w_{0}\preccurlyeq w
for every
w\in W
.

•

Tree:
if for every
w\in W
the set

\{u\in W:u\preccurlyeq w\}
is finite linearly ordered
(by
\preccurlyeq
) set.

By default we assume that
all Kripke models of
{\sf IPC}
in this paper
are finite rooted and tree.
As we will see in
section

4.2
,
some other sort of Kripke semantics are used, called
{{\sf AR}_{{\sf par}}}\!\,
-models,
which might not be finite or tree.
Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
, we define
\textup{Mod}(A)
as the class of all
(finite rooted tree) Kripke models of
A
.

2.4
{\sf NNIL}
propositions

The class of
No Nested Implications in the Left
,

{\sf NNIL}
formulae, was discovered by Albert Visser and first
published in

[
Visser et al., 1995
]
, and more explored in
[
Visser, 2002
;
Ilin et al., 2020
]
.
For simplicity of notations, we may write
{\sf N}
for
{\sf NNIL}
.
The crucial
result of
[
Visser, 2002
]
is to provide
an algorithm that
takes
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
and returns its best

{\sf NNIL}
approximation
A^{*}
from below, i.e.,
\vdash A^{*}\rightarrow A
and
for all
{\sf NNIL}
formulae
B
such that
\vdash B\rightarrow A
, we have

\vdash B\rightarrow A^{*}
.
Later in this paper we define another algorithm
A^{\star}

which calculates the best
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
-approximation of
A
from below
(
section

4.5
).
The classes
{\sf NNIL}
and
{\sf NI}
of propositions
in
\mathcal{L}_{0}
are defined inductively:

•

a\in{\sf NNIL}
and
a\in{\sf NI}
for every
a\in{\sf atom}
.

•

B\circ C\in{\sf NNIL}
if
B,C\in{\sf NNIL}
.
Also
B\circ C\in{\sf NI}
if
B,C\in{\sf NI}
. (
\circ\in\{\vee,\wedge\}
)

•

B\to C\in{\sf NNIL}
if
B\in{\sf NI}
and
C\in{\sf NNIL}
.

2.5
Notations on sets of propositions

In rest of the paper we deal with several sets of propositions and following notations make life easier.
Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
, let
{\sf sub}(A)
be the set of all subformulas of
A
.
For simplicity of notations, we write

{\sf X_{1}\ldots X_{n}}
for
{\sf X_{1}}\cap\ldots\cap{\sf X_{n}}
, when

{\sf X_{i}}
are sets of propositions.
For a set
\Gamma
of propositions define

•

\Gamma^{\vee}:=\{\bigvee\Delta:\Delta\subseteq_{\text{fin}}\Gamma\text{ and }\Delta\neq\emptyset\}.
(
X\subseteq_{\text{fin}}Y
indicates that
X
is a finite subset of
Y
)

•

\Gamma(X)
indicates the set
\Gamma\cap\mathcal{L}_{0}(X)
.

•

{{\downarrow}^{\!\!{\sf T}}\!\Gamma}:=
the class of all

\Gamma
-projective propositions in
{\sf T}
.
We say that a proposition
A
is
\Gamma
-projective in
{\sf T}
,
if there is some substitution
\theta
and
B\in\Gamma
such that

{\sf T}\vdash\theta(A)\leftrightarrow B
and
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}x\leftrightarrow\theta(x)
for every
x\in{\sf var}
(see
section

3.1
).
Whenever
{\sf T}={\sf IPC}
, we may omit the superscript
{\sf T}
and simply write
{\downarrow}{\Gamma}
.

Also define

•

{\sf N}:={\sf NNIL}:=
as defined in
section

2.4
.

•

{\sf P}^{\sf T}:={\sf Prime}^{\sf T}:=
the set of all

{\sf T}
-prime propositions, i.e. the set of propositions
A

such that for every
B,C
with
{\sf T}\vdash A\to(B\vee C)

either we have
{\sf T}\vdash A\to B
or
{\sf T}\vdash A\to C
.
Whenever
{\sf T}={\sf IPC}
, we may omit
the
{\sf T}
-superscript from notations.

And finally we assume that

(.)^{\vee}
has the lowest precedence after
{\downarrow}{(.)}
. This means that

{\downarrow}{XY}^{\vee}:=\left({\downarrow}{\left(XY\right)}\right)^{\vee}.

2.6
Admissibility and preservativity

Given a Logic
{\sf T}
, the binary relation
\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${}$}\end{picture}}

is defined to hold for those pairs
A
and
B
such that the inference
rule
A/B
is admissible. More precisely
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${}$}\end{picture}}B
iff
for every substitution
\theta
,
{\sf T}\vdash\theta(A)
implies

{\sf T}\vdash\theta(B)
.
The admissibility relationship is trivial when one considers the classical
propositional logic, since every admissible
A/B
is also derivable.
However this relationship is highly nontrivial when one considers
a modal logic or intuitionistic logic. Probably the first known
non-derivable admissible rule is the following rule
[
Harrop, 1960
]
:

\neg A\to(B\vee C)

(\neg A\to B)\vee(\neg A\to C)

Harvey Friedman asked in 1975 for decidability of admissibility
in the intuitionistic propositional logic. Then

[
Rybakov, 1987b
;
Rybakov, 1992
;
Rybakov, 1997
]
answers to this question
positively. Although it was shown that no finite base exists for all
admissible rules of the intuitionistic logic
{\sf IPC}

[
Rybakov, 1987a
]
,
de Jongh and Visser introduced a recursive base and conjectured it to generate all admissible rules of
{\sf IPC}
. Then Iemhoff proved this
conjecture
[
Iemhoff, 2001a
;
Iemhoff, 2001b
]
.

Here in this paper, we consider a relativised version of admissibility.
Given a logic
{\sf T}
and a set
\Gamma
of propositions define
the
\Gamma
-admissibility relation in
{\sf T}
as follows

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
iff for every substitution
\theta

and
C\in\Gamma
:

{\sf T}\vdash\theta(C\to A)

implies
{\sf T}\vdash\theta(C\to B)
.

Note that there is a hidden role for the language
\mathcal{L}_{0}

in the definition of
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
, when we consider substitution
\theta
.
However since almost everywhere in the paper we fix the language
\mathcal{L}_{0}
,
by default we assume substitutions over this fixed language and we do not explicitly mention
\mathcal{L}_{0}
.

There is also another binary relation on propositions, called
preservativity, which is known .
The
\Gamma
-preservativity relation in
{\sf T}
is defined
as follows:

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B\quad\text{ iff }\quad\forall\,E\in\Gamma({\sf T}\vdash E\to A\Rightarrow{\sf T}\vdash E\to B).

Preservativity could be considered as intuitionistic analogue of classical
interpretability or conservativity.
This notion as a propositional logic, well studied in

[
Visser, 2002
]
and
[
Iemhoff, 2003
]
provided
Kripke semantics for it.
[
Zhou, 2003
;
Iemhoff et al., 2005
]
include some
more elaboration on preservativity and provability, including fixed-point theorem and Beth property.

Following theorem says that
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
and
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
are ascending
on
\Gamma
. All over this paper we may
use this fact without mentioning.

Theorem 2.1
.

If
\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma^{\prime}

then
{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma^{\prime}}$}\end{picture}}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}
and

{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma^{\prime}}$}\end{picture}}}\subseteq{\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}
.

Proof.

Left to the reader.
∎

Theorem 2.2
.

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
implies
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-1.2){ ${{{\downarrow}^{\!\!{\sf T}}\!\Gamma}}$}\end{picture}}B
.

Proof.

Let
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
and
E\in{{\downarrow}^{\!\!{\sf T}}\!\Gamma}
such that
{\sf T}\vdash E\to A
.
Since
E\in{{\downarrow}^{\!\!{\sf T}}\!\Gamma}
there is some
\theta
and
E^{\dagger}\in\Gamma
such that

E\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\theta(a)\leftrightarrow a
for every
a\in{\sf atom}
and

{\sf T}\vdash\theta(E)\leftrightarrow E^{\dagger}
.
Hence

{\sf T}\vdash E^{\dagger}\to\theta(A)
. Then by
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B

we get
{\sf T}\vdash E^{\dagger}\to\theta(B)
and thus

E\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}\theta(E\to B)
. Since
\theta
is
E
-projective,
we may conclude
E\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\vdash$}\put(0.1,1.2){ $$}\put(0.1,-0.4){ ${{\sf T}}$}\end{picture}}E\to B
and thus
{\sf T}\vdash E\to B
.
∎

Question 1
.

What can be said about the other direction of
theorem

2.2
?

Remark 2.3
.

By
theorems

2.2
and
2.1
,
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
implies

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
, however the converse may not hold. As a counterexample
let
A
and
B
are two different variables and
\top\in\Gamma

and
{\sf T}={\sf IPC}
. Then we have
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
and not
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
.

Later in this paper we axiomatize
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
and
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}

for several pairs
({\sf T},\Gamma)
. Before we continue with this, let us see some basic axioms.

Let
{\sf T}
be a logic.
The logic
[{\sf T}]

proves statements
A\rhd B
for
A,B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}

and has the following axioms and rules:

Aximos

{\sf Ax}:

A\rhd B
,
for every
{\sf T}\vdash A\to B
.

Rules

A\rhd B

A\rhd C

Conj

A\rhd B\wedge C

A\rhd B

B\rhd C

Cut

A\rhd C

The above mentioned axiom and rules are not interesting, because

[{\sf T}]\vdash A\rhd B
iff
{\sf T}\vdash A\to B
. However we define several interesting additional rules:

B\rhd A

C\rhd A

Disj

B\vee C\rhd A

A\rhd B

(
C\in\Delta
)

\textup{Mont}(\Delta)

C\to A\rhd C\to B

Theorem 2.4

(
Soundness
)
.

If
{\sf T}
is closed under substitutions, then
[{\sf T}]
is sound for relative admissibility
interpretations,
i.e.
[{\sf T}]\vdash A\rhd B
implies
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
and
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B

for every set

\Gamma
of propositions and every logic
{\sf T}
. Moreover

1.

if
\Gamma
is
{\sf T}
-prime i.e.
{\sf T}\vdash A\to(B\vee C)

implies either
{\sf T}\vdash A\to B
or
{\sf T}\vdash A\to C
for every

A\in\Gamma
and arbitrary
B,C
, and

\Gamma
is closed under substitutions, then Disj is also sound,

2.

if
\Gamma
is closed under
\Delta
-conjunctions, i.e.
A\in\Gamma
and
B\in\Delta
implies
A\wedge B\in\Gamma

(
up to
{\sf T}
-provable equivalence relation
)
, then
\textup{Mont}(\Delta)

is sound.

Proof.

Easy induction on the complexity of proof
[{\sf T}]\vdash A\rhd B

and left to the reader.
∎

Theorem 2.5
.

{\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}={\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma^{\vee}}$}\end{picture}}\ }
and
{\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}={\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma^{\vee}}$}\end{picture}}}\ }
.

Proof.

We only show
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
iff
A\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma^{\vee}}$}\end{picture}}B
and leave the similar argument for
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
iff
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma^{\vee}}$}\end{picture}}}\ B
to the reader.
The right-to-left direction holds since

\Gamma\subseteq\Gamma^{\vee}
. For the other direction assume that

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
and let
E\in\Gamma^{\vee}
such that

{\sf T}\vdash E\to A
. Then
E=\bigvee_{i}E_{i}
with
E_{i}\in\Gamma
.
Hence for every
i
we have
{\sf T}\vdash E_{i}\to A
.
Then
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
implies
{\sf T}\vdash E_{i}\to B
. Thus

{\sf T}\vdash E\to B
, as desired.
∎

Notation.
Whenever
{\sf T}={\sf IPC}
we may omit the
{\sf T}

form notations
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
and
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
and simply write

\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){\line{0}{1}{1.65}}\put(0.0,0.3){{\footnotesize{$\sim$}}}\end{picture}}\!$}\put(-0.3,1.2){ ${}$}\put(-0.3,-0.5){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
and
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
for them. Also if
\Gamma:=\{\top,\bot\}
we may omit

\Gamma
from notations.

2.7
Greatest lower bounds

Given a set
\Gamma\cup\{A\}

of propositions, and a logic

{\sf T}
, we say that
B
is a lower bound for

A
w.r.t.
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
, if the following conditions met:

1.

B\in\Gamma
,

2.

{\sf T}\vdash B\to A
.

Moreover we say that
B
is the greatest
lower bound (glb) for
A
w.r.t.
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
,
if for every lower bound
B^{\prime}
for
A
w.r.t.
(\Gamma,{\sf T})

we have
{\sf T}\vdash B^{\prime}\to B
. Note that up to
{\sf T}
-provable equivalence relation, such glb is unique
and we annotate it as

\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.

We say that
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
is downward compact, if
every
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
has glb w.r.t.
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
.

Question 2
.

One may similarly define the notion of least upper bounds
and upward compactness. Does downward compactness
imply upward compactness?

Theorem 2.6
.

B
is the glb for
A
w.r.t.
(\Gamma,{\sf T})
, iff

•

B\in\Gamma
,

•

{\sf T}\vdash B\to A
,

•

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
.

Hence we have

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.

Proof.

Left to the reader.
∎

Question 3
.

As we saw in
theorem

2.6
, the glb may be expressed via
preservativity relation
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
. One may think of its adjoint relation
which best suites for lub’s:

A\mathrel{{}^{{}^{*}}\!\!\!\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}B\quad\text{ iff }\quad\forall\,E\in\Gamma({\sf T}\vdash A\to E\Rightarrow{\sf T}\vdash B\to E).

[
Visser, 2002
, Corollary 7.2]
axiomatizes
\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}
for
{\sf T}={\sf IPC}
and
\Gamma={\sf NNIL}
. We ask for an axiomatization for
\mathrel{{}^{{}^{*}}\!\!\!\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}}
when we let
{\sf T}={\sf IPC}
and

\Gamma={\sf NNIL}
.

Corollary 2.7
.

If
\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
exists, then for every
B\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
we have

{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\to B\quad\text{ iff }\quad A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B.

Proof.

First assume that
{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
. Also let
E\in\Gamma

such that
{\sf T}\vdash E\to A
.
Theorem

2.6
implies

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
and hence
{\sf T}\vdash E\to\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}
.
Then by
{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\to B
we get

{\sf T}\vdash E\to B
, as desired.

For the other direction let

A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B
. By definition we have
\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\in\Gamma

and
{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\to A
. Hence by
A\mathrel{\mathrel{\begin{picture}\put(0.0,0.0){$\mid\!\approx$}\put(-0.1,1.3){ ${{\sf T}}$}\put(-0.1,-0.8){ ${\Gamma}$}\end{picture}}}B

we get
{\sf T}\vdash\lfloor{A}\rfloor^{{}^{\!{\sf T}}}_{{}_{\!{\Gamma}}}\to B
, as desired.
∎

3
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
-fication: unification to
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}

Silvio Ghilardi, in
[
Ghilardi, 1999
]
characterizes
projective propositions in the language
\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf var})

with the aid of Kripke semantics. Then he uses this characterization to prove that the unification type of

{\sf IPC}
is finitary. Afterwards, Rosalie Iemhoff

[
Iemhoff, 2001b
;
Iemhoff, 2001a
]
uses this
result together with a special sort of Kripke models,
called AR-models,
to characterize the admissible rules of
{\sf IPC}
.
In this section we consider a relativised version for those results.
The difference from previous version is that
we are not allowed to substitute parameters (a reserved set of atomics), and also instead of unification, we expect to
simplify the proposition to a

{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
proposition, called
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
-fication.
In fact, previous results will be an special case of ours when

{\sf par}=\emptyset
and hence
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}=\{\top,\bot\}
.
The methods of our proof follows main roads took in
[
Ghilardi, 1999
;
Iemhoff, 2001a
]
.

We start with relativised version of projective unification
(
section

3.1
) and extension property
(
section

3.2
).
Then (
section

3.3
)
we prove a correspondence between relativised
projectivity and extendibility.
Having such Kripke semantical charcterization in hand,
then we prove that every proposition has a finitary projective approximation (
section

3.4
).
Actually we prove something more: every proposition
has a finitary projective resolution (
section

3.4
).
Finally at the end of this section (
section

3.5
), we prove that
in the specific case, when
A\in{\sf NNIL}
, this finitary projective
resolution takes an elegant form.

3.1
Relative Projectivity

Given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
, a substitution
\theta
is
called
A
-projective
(in
{\sf IPC}
)
if

(3.1)

\text{For all atomic $a$ we have }\quad A\vdash a\leftrightarrow\theta(a).

When one considers unification for propositional logics,
projectivity is proved to be of great help
[
Ghilardi, 1997
]
.
As we will see, our study is not an exception.

If
\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf par})
, a substitution
\theta

is a
\Gamma
-fier
(as a generalization for uni-fier) for

A
, if

\vdash\theta(A)\in\Gamma\quad\text{i.e.~$\theta(A)$ is ${\sf IPC}$-equivalent to some
$A^{\prime}\in\Gamma$.}

In this case we use the notation
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.00002pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\twoheadrightarrow$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.00002pt}}\Gamma
. If

\Gamma
is a singleton
\{A^{\prime}\}
we write

A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.00002pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\twoheadrightarrow$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.00002pt}}A^{\prime}
instead of
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.00002pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\twoheadrightarrow$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.00002pt}}\{A^{\prime}\}
.

\theta
is a unifier for
A
if it is
\{\top\}
-fier for
A
.
We say that a substitution
\theta
projects
A
to
\Gamma

(notation:
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}\Gamma
)
if
\theta
is
A
-projective and
\Gamma
-fier.
We say that
A
is
\Gamma
-projective (notation

A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.389pt}}\Gamma
) if there is some
\theta
such that

A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}\Gamma
.
We say that

A
is projective, if it is
\{\top\}
-projective.
Also
{\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{\,}}}{\Gamma}

indicates the set of all propositions which are

\Gamma
-projective.

Uniqueness of
\Gamma
-projections

Let
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}A^{\prime}
and
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.84393pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\tau$}}\hskip-1.84393pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.389pt}}A^{\prime\prime}

and
A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}\in\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf par})
.
From the
A
-projectivity of
\theta
and
\tau
,
for every atomic
a
we have
A\vdash\theta(a)\leftrightarrow\tau(a)
. Hence
A\vdash\theta(A)\leftrightarrow\tau(A)

and then
A\vdash A^{\prime}\leftrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
. By applying

\theta
to both sides of this derivation,
we have
\theta(A)\vdash\theta(A^{\prime})\leftrightarrow\theta(A^{\prime\prime})
.
Since
\theta
is identity over parameters and
A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime}\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf par})
,
we have
A^{\prime}\vdash A^{\prime}\leftrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
.
Hence
\vdash A^{\prime}\to A^{\prime\prime}
. Similarly we have

\vdash A^{\prime\prime}\to A^{\prime}
, and hence
\vdash A^{\prime}\leftrightarrow A^{\prime\prime}
.
This argument shows that for every
\Gamma
-projective

A
, there is a unique (modulo
{\sf IPC}
-provable
equivalence)
A^{\dagger}\in\Gamma
such that
for some
A
-projective substitution
\theta

we have
\vdash\theta(A)\leftrightarrow A^{\dagger}
. Such unique

A^{\dagger}
is called the
\Gamma
-projection

of
A
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
A
be
\Gamma
-projective and
A^{\dagger}\in\Gamma

its projection. Then
\vdash A\to A^{\dagger}
.

Proof.

Let
\theta
be the
A
-projective
\Gamma
-fier for

A
, i.e.
A\vdash B\leftrightarrow\theta(B)
for every
B
,
and
\vdash\theta(A)\leftrightarrow A^{\dagger}
.
Hence we have
A\vdash A\leftrightarrow\theta(A)
and thus
A\vdash A\leftrightarrow A^{\dagger}
.
This implies
\vdash A\to A^{\dagger}
, as desired.
∎

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
\Gamma\subseteq\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf par})
.
If
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}A^{\dagger}\in\Gamma

and
B
is an arbitrary proposition,
then we have

\vdash A\to B\quad\text{ iff }\quad\vdash\theta(A^{\dagger}\to B).

Proof.

The left-to-right direction is obvious. For other direction,
let
\vdash\theta(A^{\dagger}\to B)
. Hence

A\vdash\theta(A^{\dagger}\to B)
and then
A\vdash A^{\dagger}\to B
.

Section

3.1
implies
\vdash A\to A^{\dagger}

and thus
\vdash A\to B
.
∎

w\in W

\mathcal{K}_{w}

Lemma 3.3
.

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\preceq_{1}^{{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern is equivalent to
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\preceq^{{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern .

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\preceq^{{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}}}}\mathcal{K}
and
R
is a relation with above mentioned properties. It is enough to define a function
f\subseteq R
such that
f
and
R

share the same domain
W^{\prime}
.
For every
\preccurlyeq^{\prime}
-minimal node
w^{\prime}\in W^{\prime}
,
define
f(w^{\prime})
an arbitrary node
w
with
w^{\prime}\mathrel{R}w
. Note that such
w
always exists. Since
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
is tree, for every
w^{\prime}_{1}\in W^{\prime}

which is not minimal, there is a unique predecessor

w^{\prime}_{0}\preccurlyeq^{\prime}w^{\prime}_{1}
. Then by definition for every
w^{\prime}_{1}\in W^{\prime}
there is some

w_{1}\in W
such that
f(w^{\prime}_{0})\preccurlyeq w_{1}
. Define
f(w^{\prime}_{1}):=w_{1}

for some such
w_{1}
. Then it is not difficult to observe that
this
f
satisfies the condition
“
w^{\prime}\preccurlyeq^{\prime}v^{\prime}
implies
f(w^{\prime})\preccurlyeq f(v^{\prime})
”.
∎

Although
\subseteq^{{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}}}}
is not equivalent to

\preceq_{1}^{{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}}}}
, we have the following partial equivalency:

Lemma 3.4
.

If
\mathcal{K}_{0}\preceq_{1}^{{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}}}}\mathcal{K}_{1}\Vdash A
then

\mathcal{K}_{0}\subseteq^{{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}}}}\mathcal{K}_{2}\Vdash A
for some
\mathcal{K}_{2}
.

Proof.

The proof is almost identical to the proof of theorem 6.9 in

[
Visser et al., 1995
]
and we refer the
reader to it for more details.
Let
\mathcal{K}_{i}=(W_{i},\preccurlyeq_{i},\mathrel{V}_{i})
for
i\in\{0,1\}

and
f:W_{0}\to W_{1}
be the embedding of
\mathcal{K}_{0}
in
\mathcal{K}_{1}
.
Moreover we may assume that
f
is surjective, lest
we add a copy of
\mathcal{K}_{1}
to
\mathcal{K}_{0}

with a fresh root in beneath of them and then extend
the embedding on the new nodes.

Define
\mathcal{K}_{2}:=(W_{2},\preccurlyeq_{2},\mathrel{V}_{2})
as follows:

•

W_{2}:=\{(w_{0},f(w_{0}),w_{1}):w_{0}\in W_{0}\text{ and }f(w_{0})\preccurlyeq_{1}w_{1}\in W_{1}\}
.

•

(w_{0},f(w_{0}),w_{1})\preccurlyeq_{2}(w^{\prime}_{0},f(w^{\prime}_{0}),w^{\prime}_{1})
iff either of the following holds:

–

w_{0}\preccurlyeq_{0}w^{\prime}_{0}
and
w_{1}=f(w_{0})
,

–

w_{0}=w^{\prime}_{0}
and
w_{1}\preccurlyeq_{1}w^{\prime}_{1}
.

•

(w_{0},f(w_{0}),w_{1})\mathrel{V}_{2}a
iff
w_{1}\mathrel{V}_{1}a
.

It is straightforward to show that
\mathcal{K}_{2}
is a finite rooted
tree-frame Kripke model with the root
(\rho,f(\rho),f(\rho))

in which
\rho
is the root of
\mathcal{K}_{0}
.
Also

\mathcal{K}_{1}
and
\mathcal{K}_{2}
are bisimilar and hence prove the same set of propositions including
A
.
Moreover one may easily show that
g
as defined in the following,
is a 1-1 embedding of
\mathcal{K}_{0}
into
\mathcal{K}_{2}
:

g(w_{0}):=(w_{0},f(w_{0}),f(w_{0}))
.
∎

Remember that

indicate
{\sf NNIL}\cap\mathcal{L}_{0}({\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern .
The following theorem is a Kripke semantical
characterization of
{\sf NNIL}
propositions

[
Visser et al., 1995
]
.

Theorem 3.5
.

Given
{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern , we have

Remark 3.6
.

Modulo
{\sf IPC}
-provable equivalence,

{\sf NNIL}
is finite.

Proof.

Observe that each proposition can be written
as
\bigvee\bigwedge C
in which
C
is an atomic or implication, which we call it a component.
Observe that the number of propositions in
n
atomics,

f(n)
, is less than or equal to
2^{2^{g(n)}}
, in which
g(n)
is the
number of components in
n
atomics. Then observe that

g(n+1)\leq(n+1)f(n)+n+1
, because one may assume that
each component is either of the form
p\to A
for some
atomic
p
and some
A
in
n
variables, or it is of the
form
p
for some atomic
p
. Hence the following recursive function is an upperbound for the
number of all formulas in
n
atomics:

f(0):=2\quad\quad,\quad\quad f(n+1):=2^{2^{(n+1)(f(n)+1)}}\qed

Define
[\![{\mathcal{K}}]\!]_{{}_{\Gamma}}:=\{A\in\Gamma:\mathcal{K}\Vdash A\}.

[

, theorem 7.1.2]
.

Given a substitution

\theta

and a Kripke model
\mathcal{K}=(W,\preccurlyeq,\mathrel{V})
, we define

\theta(\mathcal{K}):=(W,\preccurlyeq,\mathrel{V}^{\prime})

as follows.
For every atomic
a
,
define
w\mathrel{V}^{\prime}a
iff
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\theta(a)
.

Lemma 3.8
.

Given a general substitution
\theta
,
Kripke model
\mathcal{K}
and
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
, we have

\mathcal{K},w\Vdash\theta(A)\quad\quad\text{iff}\quad\quad\theta(\mathcal{K}),w\Vdash A.

Proof.

Use induction on the complexity of
A
.
∎

Remark 3.9
.

Let
\theta
and
\tau
be two general substitutions
and
(\theta\tau)
is their composition.
Above lemma, implies that

(\theta\tau)(\mathcal{K})=\tau(\theta(\mathcal{K}))
. This conflicts
with our standard notation for the composition of
functions. This confliction could be resolved
by choosing another name, e.g.
\theta^{*}
for the operation
on Kripke models corresponding to
\theta
.
However, for the simplicity of notations, we prefer not to do so.

Given Kripke models
\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
,
we say that
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
is an

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle a\hfil

-
variant
of
\mathcal{K}
, if

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{K}
share the same frame and the same
atomic valuations, except possibly at the root and only
for atomics not in

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle a\hfil

, for which we may
have different valuations. We also say that
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
is
a variant of
\mathcal{K}
,
if it is
\emptyset
-variant of
\mathcal{K}
.
Also for a Kripke model
\mathcal{K}
with the root
w_{0}
we define

\mathcal{K}\Vdash^{-}A
iff
\mathcal{K},w\Vdash A
for
every
w\neq w_{0}
.

We say that a proposition
A
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle a\hfil

-extendible
if for every Kripke model
\mathcal{K}\Vdash A
and every

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\subseteq^{{{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle a\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern -variant
\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}
of

For later applications in this paper it is helpful to define

{\sf par}
-extendibility also for a class of Kripke models.

is a class of Kripke models.

as the disjoint union of all Kripke models in
\mathscr{K}
with a fresh root

such that for every atomic

we have

\sum(\mathscr{K}),w_{0}\Vdash a
iff
\mathscr{K}\Vdash a
.

3.3
{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})
-projectivity and
{\sf par}
-extendibility

In this section we will prove
theorem

3.12
,
an extension of Ghilardi’s
characterization of projective propositions
via the notion of extendibility
(see
theorem

3.10
).

For a proposition
A
and
a set

The following theorem, is the main preliminary tool
provided in
[
Ghilardi, 1999
]
to characterize the unification type of
{\sf IPC}
. We refer the reader to

[
Ghilardi, 1999
, theorem 5]
for its proof.
We will prove a generalization of this in
theorem

3.12
.

Theorem 3.10
.

For
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
,
the following conditions are equivalent:

1.

{\theta_{\!\!A}}
is a unifier for
A
,
i.e.
\vdash{\theta_{\!\!A}}(A)
,

2.

A
is projective,

3.

A
is extendible.

Before we continue with a generalization
of above theorem, let us give another definition.
Let
\mathcal{K}
be a Kripke model and

{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern .
Then define
A^{\dagger}
as follows: (remember that previously we defined

A^{\dagger}
for
{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
-projective
A
as the unique

such that

{\sf IPC}
-provable equivalence relation.)

0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}},A}\bigwedge[\![{\mathcal{K}}]\!]_{{}_{{{\sf N}({\sf par})}}}\right)

Note that since by
section

3.2
the set

{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
is finite and
[\![{\mathcal{K}}]\!]_{{}_{{{\sf N}({\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern ,
the conjunction
\bigwedge[\![{\mathcal{K}}]\!]_{{}_{{{\sf N}({\sf par})}}}
is
a proposition and also the disjunction may considered as a finite disjunction.

Remark 3.11
.

Note that by above definition,
if
\vdash A\to B
then
\vdash A^{\dagger}\to B^{\dagger}
.

Theorem 3.12
.

For
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
, the following conditions are equivalent:

1.

A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 5.00002pt\hskip-5.00002pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.0pt\hskip-5.0pt\hbox{$\twoheadrightarrow$}\hskip-5.0pt\hskip-3.9115pt\raisebox{6.22949pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\theta_{\!\!A}}$}}\hskip-3.9115pt\hskip 5.0pt}$}\hskip-5.00002pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-2.00555pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 5.00002pt}}A^{\dagger}
,

2.

A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.389pt}}{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
,

3.

A
is
{\sf par}
-extendible.

Proof.

1\to 2
:
From definitions of
A^{\dagger}
, evidently

A^{\dagger}\in{\sf NNIL}
.
Also observe that

\theta^{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle x\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle x\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle x\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle x\hfil$\crcr}}}}_{\!\!A}
is
A
-projective. Then
since
A
-projective
substitutions are closed under compositions,

{\theta_{\!\!A}}
are
A
-projective.

2\to 3
: Let
A\mathrel{\hbox{\hskip 6.38902pt\hskip-6.38902pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 6.389pt\hskip-6.389pt\hbox{${\rightarrowtail}\!\!\!\!\!{\twoheadrightarrow}$}\hskip-6.389pt\hskip-1.9045pt\raisebox{5.22394pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\theta$}}\hskip-1.9045pt\hskip 6.389pt}$}\hskip-6.38902pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.13539pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 6.38902pt}}A^{\prime}\in{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}

and
\mathcal{K}\Vdash A
and

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\subseteq^{{{\sf par}}}\mathcal{K}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\Vdash^{-}A

seeking some variant
\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}
of
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
such that

\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}\Vdash A
.
Let
\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}=\theta(\mathcal{K}^{\prime})
.
First note that
by
A
-projectivity of
\theta
,
\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}
is a

{\sf par}
-variant of
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
.
Since
\mathcal{K}\Vdash A^{\prime}
,

A^{\prime}\in{{\sf NNIL}({\sf par})}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\subseteq^{{{\sf par}}}\mathcal{K}
,

theorem

3.5
implies that

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\Vdash A^{\prime}
. Hence

\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\Vdash\theta(A)
, and by

section

3.2

we have
\mathcal{K}^{\prime\prime}\Vdash A
.

3\to 1
: Let
A
is
{\sf par}
-extendible and
show
\vdash A^{\dagger}\leftrightarrow{\theta_{\!\!A}}(A)
.
We use induction on the height of Kripke model
\mathcal{K}

and show
\mathcal{K}\Vdash A^{\dagger}\leftrightarrow{\theta_{\!\!A}}(A)
.
Suppose that
w_{0}
is the root of
\mathcal{K}
.
By induction hypothesis,
we have
\mathcal{K}\Vdash^{-}A^{\dagger}\leftrightarrow{\theta_{\!\!A}}(A)

and hence
by
section

3.2
,
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\Vdash^{-}A^{\dagger}\leftrightarrow A
. We show
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\Vdash A^{\dagger}\leftrightarrow A
.
If
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\nVdash^{-}A^{\dagger}
, then

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\nVdash^{-}A
and hence

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\nVdash A^{\dagger}

and

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\nVdash A
.
Then

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\Vdash A^{\dagger}\leftrightarrow A
and we are done.
So assume that
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\Vdash^{-}A\wedge A^{\dagger}
.
It is enough to show the following items:

•

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\Vdash A
implies

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\Vdash A^{\dagger}
.
By
section

3.1
we have
\vdash A\to A^{\dagger}

and hence we have desired result.

•

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\Vdash A^{\dagger}
implies

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\Vdash A
.
Let
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\Vdash A^{\dagger}
.
Also assume that

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})
is

\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr

\hfil\textstyle p\hfil

-model,
i.e.
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\Vdash{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}}
and

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\nVdash\bigvee({\sf par}\setminus{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}})
.
Since
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K}),w_{0}\Vdash A^{\dagger}
,
for some

\mathcal{K}_{1}\in\textup{Mod}(A)
with
\mathcal{K}_{1}\Vdash{\mathchoice{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\displaystyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\displaystyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\displaystyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\textstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\textstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\textstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}{\vbox{\halign{#\cr\kern-0.7pt\cr$\mkern 2.0mu\scriptscriptstyle\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraitd}$}}}\mkern-1.5mu\leaders{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\mkern 0.0mu\mathrel{\mathchoice{{}\hbox{$\displaystyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\textstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\montraita}$}}{{}\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle{\montraita}$}}}\mkern 0.0mu$}}{\hfill}\mkern-1.5mu\fldr$\crcr\kern-0.3pt\cr$\hfil\scriptscriptstyle p\hfil$\crcr}}}}

we have

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\Vdash[\![{\mathcal{K}_{1}}]\!]_{{}_{{{\sf N}({\sf par})}}}
.

Theorem

3.7
implies that

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\preceq^{{{\sf par}}}\mathcal{K}_{1}
. Then

section

3.2
implies

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\preceq_{1}^{{{\sf par}}}\mathcal{K}_{1}

and thus by
section

3.2

there is some
\mathcal{K}_{2}\Vdash A
such that

{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\subseteq^{{{\sf par}}}\mathcal{K}_{2}
. Since
A
is
{\sf par}
-extendible, there is a

{\sf par}
-variant
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}
of
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})

such that
\mathcal{K}^{\prime}\Vdash A
. Thus

section

3.3
implies
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\Vdash A
.
∎

Corollary 3.13
.

{{\sf N}({\sf par})}
-projectivity is decidable. In other words,
given
A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}
, one may algorithmically decide
A\in{{\downarrow^{\!\!{}^{\,}}}{{\sf N}({\sf par})}}
.

Proof.

Given
A
, by
theorem

3.12

it is enough to decide
{\sf IPC}\vdash{\theta_{\!\!A}}(A)\leftrightarrow A^{\dagger}
, which
is decidable since
{\sf IPC}
is decidable.
∎

Lemma 3.14
.

If
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\Vdash^{-}A
and
A
is valid in a

{\sf par}
-variant of
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})
then
{\theta_{\!\!A}}(\mathcal{K})\Vdash A
.

Proof.

See proof of the theorem 5 in
[
Ghilardi, 1999
]
.
∎

3.4
Projective resolution

The main result in
[
Ghilardi, 1999
]
is that
the unification type
of
{\sf IPC}
is finitary. It means that for every

A\in\mathcal{L}_{0}({\sf var})
, there exists
a finite
complete set of unifiers
for
A
, i.e. a finite set

\Theta
of unifiers for
A
such that
every unifier of
A
is less general than
some
\theta\in\Theta
. We say that
\theta
is less general than
\gamma
if there is some substitution

\lambda
such that for every
x\in{\sf var}
we have

\vdash\theta(x)\leftrightarrow\lambda(\gamma(x)).

The proof of above mentioned fact is based on projective approximations
which later
[
Ghilardi, 2002
]
provides a resolution/tableaux method for its computation.
The aim for this subsection is to prove a relativised version
